## v0.340.245 transport-adjacent paper credential fence

The explicit single-attempt Alpaca paper submission boundary now samples the exact caller-provided saved-pair binding after the final permission and emergency-stop checkpoint and immediately before the sole POST transport attempt. If that credential callback fails or the pair changes in the intervening checkpoint, the boundary performs no POST, closes the shared provider budget as `NOT_SENT`, and permanently leaves the prepared request original-client-ID lookup-only. Callback exceptions collapse to a fixed diagnostic without exposing private vault detail. The mandatory pacing release profile retains an injected mutation at this exact boundary.

## v0.340.244 durable order root identity fence

Default durable-order reads and writes now resolve one physical storage root and retain the configured directory's identity through emergency admission, rotation inspection, SQLite order access and linked portfolio reconciliation. Symlink roots are rejected. Replacing the configured directory during the cut check, after order-journal open or during linked read-only replay fails closed before an operation can be acknowledged against the replacement root. Existing environment-retargeting behavior still keeps an operation on its original root.

## v0.340.242 pinned emergency-intent directory durability

The independent local paper emergency latch now pins one POSIX parent-directory descriptor across creation, idempotent acknowledgement, removal and directory fsync. Latch entry operations are relative to that descriptor, and the operation refuses success if the public parent path no longer names the same directory. Deterministic replacement-race tests cover both persist and reset boundaries. Windows retains the conservative standard-library fallback because Python cannot fsync directory handles there; native Windows storage-fault acceptance remains open.

## v0.340.58 exact response-observation cold replay

After the sole permitted paper POST returns, the boundary no longer trusts the in-process observation append result. It performs two stable read-only receipt replays through the pinned request-journal binding, validates the complete current chain, locates the unique exact `OBSERVE` child of the checked marker and replays the prefix through that event. ## v0.340.24 cancelled-lifespan failure observation

If ASGI lifespan cancellation arrives while the private shutdown worker is running, the application now waits synchronously for that exact worker, retrieves its terminal result and cancels the pending asyncio wrapper before returning. Successful cleanup re-raises cancellation; cleanup failure overrides cancellation so shutdown cannot appear clean or leave an orphaned future exception. Direct tests cover both outcomes and require the private lifecycle thread to be gone with no event-loop exception report.

## v0.340.10 fresh-reservation checkpoint ownership

The reconnect coordinator previously required `duplicate_checkpoint` to be a boolean but did not require it to be false after granting this caller the sole fresh provider request. A corrupted or overlapping acknowledgement could therefore claim both that the cursor was written and that the checkpoint was a replay, then close the reconnect budget automatically. A fresh reservation now rejects duplicate checkpoint acknowledgements, retains the exact v6 checkpoint plus pending budget, and requires the established explicit no-network reconciliation path. This performs no second provider request. The v0.340.9 Windows portability and complete-history audit fixes are retained.

Final local validation passed 4,386 tests with one expected non-Windows skip and one existing Starlette/httpx warning; the reconnect-budget suite passed 325, the direct cursor/checkpoint/reconnect set passed 426, the complete Activity SSE set passed 524 and the merged Windows/audit/reconnect/release set passed 678. The shared file fingerprint compares stable identity/size/mtime/link fields across path and handle APIs, checks creation-time stability separately inside each API, and retains path-API creation time plus the content digest for later comparisons. POSIX cross-API creation-time equality remains required. Identity, single-link, sidecar, byte-cap and changed-file refusals remain intact.

## v0.340.8 transport-bound recovery closure

The primary checkpoint acknowledgement path required a transport-bound v6 cursor record, but its lost/malformed-acknowledgement recovery helper and the explicit no-network reconciliation path still accepted a reservation-bound v4 record. A current reservation could therefore be retired after a mixed-version checkpoint without durable transport evidence. Both recovery boundaries now require `transport_input_bound` to be exactly true. Legacy v1-v4 records remain readable for inspection; disagreement retains the pending reservation for explicit review and performs no second provider request.

## v0.340.6 durable checkpoint review-count binding

The complete cursor profile already journaled the review input digest and all four aggregate counts, but the reconnect acknowledgement validator checked only that returned counts were bounded and arithmetically consistent. A coherently altered replay/deduplication pair could therefore remain plausible without matching the committed checkpoint. The durable public binding now carries the journaled digest and bounded counts, and the coordinator requires exact equality before closing the reservation. A mismatch returns fixed checkpoint-state uncertainty after the original single provider request and preserves the split state for explicit no-network reconciliation.

## v0.340.5 canonical checkpoint acknowledgement boundary

The reconnect coordinator previously validated the acknowledgement fields it consumed but later copied the entire checkpoint return into its public result. An unexpected internal extension could therefore cross the boundary even though it was neither canonical nor durable evidence. The coordinator now requires an exact built-in mapping with the complete key set, fixed checkpoint policy and limitations, digest-only identity fields and bounded review-count relationships before validating the durable cursor binding. Noncanonical returns preserve the exact split state for explicit no-network reconciliation and return fixed checkpoint-state uncertainty after the original single provider request.

## v0.340.4 checkpoint transport-provenance binding

The reconnect coordinator previously required internally consistent provider-access, stream-connection, reconnect and source fields, but did not compare them with whether the call actually used the injected opener seam. A corrupted checkpoint return could flip all four fields coherently and falsely report actual provider HTTPS access while still matching the durable cursor record. The coordinator now derives the expected provenance from its own call boundary and rejects any disagreement before closing the reconnect result.

## v0.340.3 durable reservation-freshness binding

The coordinator previously proved that a returned reservation matched the exact pending journal record but trusted the returned `duplicate_reservation` and `provider_request_authorized` booleans. A corrupted replay acknowledgement could therefore relabel an existing reservation as fresh and perform another provider read. The coordinator now captures a totally validated pre-BEGIN budget view and requires both acknowledgement booleans to agree with whether that reservation already existed. A concurrent reservation appearing between the snapshot and BEGIN is rejected rather than promoted.

Final local validation passed 4,367 tests with one expected non-Windows skip and one existing Starlette/httpx warning; the reconnect-budget suite passed 317, direct cursor/reconnect passed 411 and complete Activity SSE passed 514. ## v0.340.2 authoritative result-denial preservation

The shared result-write recovery wrapper previously treated every exception from `finish` as a possibly lost acknowledgement. If the exact result had already committed, a current typed store-binding, clock, head, confirmation or other state denial could therefore be replaced by the older terminal result's exact cold replay. The wrapper now permits typed recovery only for `ALPACA_ACTIVITY_SSE_RECONNECT_BUDGET_RESULT_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_UNCERTAIN`. Generic transport loss and malformed returned acknowledgements still recover only through complete validated replay. Four integration regressions cover successful checkpointing, failed-provider scheduling, trusted-time uncertainty retirement and explicit no-network checkpoint reconciliation.

Final local validation passed 4,366 tests with one expected non-Windows skip and one existing Starlette/httpx warning; the reconnect-budget suite passed 316, direct cursor/reconnect passed 398 and complete Activity SSE passed 513. ## v0.340.1 production fixed-diagnostic recovery match

The safety-closure recovery wrapper previously compared a typed error with an unprefixed code even though the production `_fail` boundary always emits the fixed `ALPACA_ACTIVITY_SSE_RECONNECT_BUDGET_` prefix. A closure whose terminal write committed and then raised its own typed acknowledgement-uncertainty diagnostic could therefore bypass the exact recovery attempted for a generic lost channel. The wrapper now matches only that complete fixed diagnostic before cold-replaying the exact requested terminal record. Other typed denials are still re-raised without recovery, so an older terminal state cannot override current confirmation, head, clock, stop or state failures.

## v0.340.0 no-network safety-closure acknowledgement recovery

No-network reconnect safety closures now treat a structurally returned acknowledgement as transport rather than durable evidence. After the outcome-clock uncertainty close, pending emergency-stop abort or retry-wait emergency-stop close returns, the wrapper ignores the tuple and cold-replays the private reconnect-budget journal. Only the exact requested terminal record may continue; an uncommitted forged return, a wrong terminal record or a same-inode rewrite remains fail-closed. These functions have no credential, cursor or provider boundary and grant no retry authority.

## Historical continuity boundary

This file intentionally retains the current v0.340 continuity window rather than duplicating the repository's complete release history. Older checkpoint prose remains recoverable from Git, versioned `validation/release_trials_*.json` evidence, `ROADMAP.md`, `VERSIONING.md`, and `LAUNCH_DRIVE.md`. Do not infer acceptance from an omitted historical paragraph.

For every future sprint, recover the latest remote development head and current CI first; use isolated work and an expected-head lease; keep engagement permissions OFF by default; preserve bounded limits, account checks, fail-closed exits, the independent emergency stop, beginner tabs, and black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help. Never publish private credentials, account data, or retained market evidence. No broker, funds, live, cloud, release, or owner authority follows from synthetic tests, local hashes, version numbers, or this compacted continuity record.
